B It is necessary to observe this too, regarding the vain speech of the barbarian, that he clearly knows and proclaims the demons by name, but he pretends, as it were, to call them abominable, along with their leader, the devil, in order that by this means he might be able to catch the simple-minded, persuading them that his myth-making is not from the evil spirit, but from one who rebukes the demons. Having advanced a little in these foolish trifles, he thinks he is showing himself to be preferable to all and immune from condemnation, and that he is teaching these things for the correction of others, while he slanders Jews and Christians who are arrayed against one another; and he maintains that those who stand for one opinion, thinking it superior to the other, have more deserved divine familiarity and the delights of Paradise. Afterwards, he says that both have fallen from their hope on account of the error inherent in each doctrine; but he declares himself alone to be the heir of Paradise, without being asked. He has done something like this: if some people, for example, were arguing among themselves about a talent, and he were to push himself into the middle and say, "This belongs to none of you because you are quarreling with one another; but it has been determined for me, as I have done nothing and have not struggled with anyone." And he who keeps condemning the one who is quarreling with the other is not ashamed to do this himself against the two.
36. And as for what proof he has that he received this book from God, it is not necessary to say, and he even calls down curses upon himself—in this one thing truly doing well—and attaches countless condemnations and reproaches to himself if he has not been sent by God, evidently hoping to cover over the suspicion of a lie. He swears by the sun and the moon, and the stars, and by the lampyris—beasts—and by runner-dogs, and by trees, and by other unknown barbaric names (which I believe are in fact the demons, and thus he swears by them).
The wretched man, moreover, having forgotten what he has uttered, a little later says: “Those who are in doubt about the scriptures are in deep apostasy.” A Could anything be found more shameless or more senseless than this? For just as an ape, while imitating the onslaught of a lion, is convicted of making a simian and not a leonine leap, so this miserable man, having attributed to himself the dignity of the highest lawgiving, is caught having nothing more than the name of the most inferior absurdity. For he has put on not only the name, but (what is more) the very form and likeness of godliness without the reality itself. Being desirous, therefore, to impose the law of fasting upon the wretched barbarians, he says this: “The month is Ramadan, in which the reading was brought down unto you; fast it. You may have intercourse with your wives at night; for they are raiment for you, and you are raiment for them. God knew that you were defrauding your souls in the fast, so He has been indulgent to you. Consort with them for solace; and eat and drink in the evening until the black thread seems to you to be white by day. And again observe the fast until the evening. And do not consort with them while you remain in the oratory.” This is the law of God! And do not approach them. Is there not as much difference between a lion and an ape, as there is between this person and the one who has legislated the divine Scripture in a filthy and cursed manner?
40. As for this lawless fasting, he incites them to the war that is waged against us, saying: “Kill them wherever you encounter them.” Then, establishing the law of charity among them as is proper, he says: “And whoever nurtures wrath against you, treat him as he has treated you.” As for self-denial and temperance, he falsely exhorts them to these, saying: “Your wives are your fields; enter your fields from whence you wish, and unite with your souls—that is, fulfill the desire of your souls.” Is this life not beastly, or rather diabolical? Recalling the dismissal of a wife by a man, he speaks thus to them: “If anyone puts away his wife, it shall not be lawful for him to take her back until she has been united to another man; and if the second puts her away, there is no condemnation in them if they return to one another; for these, he says, are the laws of God.” O the barbaric debauchery!
40. Furthermore, inciting the barbarians to homicide, he seems to bring forward the historical account as an enticement to them.
to be set before them, and to confuse the persons, as he always does, and having changed them (54), he attributes those things which were done by Joshua to Gideon; and again he attributes the deeds of Gideon to Saul; and he says that David was a contemporary of theirs (55). He claimed that the Ark was carried by angels, and not by men; thus, he knew and taught the divine Scripture, and was an imposter and godless! Since the Lord blinds the minds of his own worshipers in such a way that he proceeds exactly as he behaved, he might be led astray. For there is no need for force in matters of faith; indeed, what is to be rightly admitted, is known from the false contrary, as it is said (56): “There is no compulsion in faith, for it was made manifest that what is acceptable is distinct from the erroneous.” Indeed, he often introduces Abraham in his fables, foolishly conversing with God and disputing with Him about the resurrection of the dead; and about the four birds which were placed, as mentioned above, on the four mountains, and were then recalled, from which Abraham received the faith of the resurrection: of which matter the most ancient Scripture makes no mention. And yet, he does not sin only by being shameless, but he also seems to disapprove of usury and encourages the giving of alms to the poor; so that, appearing to be a good counselor, he might secretly inflict mortal harm, the wretch! And in these things, after he has prayed for victory over us, he puts an end to his first piece of nonsense.
REFUTATION II
41. The second chapter of the fable of Muhammad is inscribed to the family of Amran. Right from the beginning, he affirms that this scripture was given by God, and adds the cause of this thing, namely, that it is confirmed by that previous time, that is to say, the Law and the Gospel, as a guide to men and salvation. Observe, therefore, whether according to this promise the support of the Law and the Gospel subsequently shows itself to be true, and whether those things lying in the scripture are excellent and sound; or whether, being defective, they need supplementation. Because, if there is nothing of this kind in it, or if it is inverted, he certainly lies, and is convicted of uttering these things with a deceitful mouth, and of originating from the father of lies. Therefore, he jokes at length in what follows about a certain maid of Amran, who supposedly came from somewhere; and he relates her prayer and supplication for the conception of Mary, who, he says, was the Mother of Jesus; and that she presented this [Mother] in the temple and she was accepted by the Lord, and received by Zechariah. For this reason, when he entered the temple, and beheld her there by divine provision, he asked from whence she came. He says that she replied that God had spoken; that she herself had requested a child of God there; and that a promise was made to her that she would have a child, John, a just man and a prophet. He immediately, without doubt, how...
he should have a son in his old age, received a penalty of silence for three days. Then he brings in the angels saying to Mary: God has chosen you before all women. But as for the narration to Mohammed of this unknown matter, he says as he who speaks to him says: "What we brought down to you, you were not there; when the angels explained whose Mary was to be." But you were not there. The angels, however, are said to have told him: God, he says, sends glad tidings to you of his Word, Christ Jesus, the son of Mary, existing in this life and in the future, one of those nearest to God, conversing with men even from infancy, and from among the just. Then he introduces her as wanting to know how she could have a son without a man. To them, he says: "What God wills, that happens." And he does not say that he told the angels about the wisdom and the Law and the Gospel, and that he was sent as an apostle to the children of Israel. Then he introduces Christ as having made birds from clay, and having breathed life into them, and as healing the blind and lepers, and resurrecting the dead, and as all-knowing; and that he called his own God the God of men; and that he chose for himself the apostles; and that God said to him: "I will subject you to death, and I will raise you up to me, and I will purify you from those who deny; and I will establish those who have followed you above those who have denied, on the day of resurrection." But he will punish those who have denied with a heavy punishment in the future, and there is no helper for them. But the nature of the likeness: Jesus, he says, is like the nature of the first Adam whom God formed. And he adds saying: This is the true narration.
42. Having said this that is true, he undoubtedly rejects the history of the divine Gospels; for if among the divine Gospels the mother of the Theotokos is not proclaimed by Jambra, what shall we do with those who are placed in Luke, the nine months of silence of Zacharias, and the three days only which he says this happened for; where are the birds fashioned and animated by Jesus from clay? But as it is, how truly these things are invented from an unknown source by his madness; this is the confirmatory writing of those before it; but it seems he has forgotten what he promised; and perhaps he does mischief here, even if it does not escape us. For since two things are proposed to him concerning our Lord Jesus, which he strives to demonstrate, one is that he in no way came, and the other is that he denies that, even if Christ did come, he was God, and wishes him to have been nothing but a man, as the first way D
of refutation seems too evident to him, he flees to the second; and having run through the history of the incarnation in such a way, he hoped to overturn his divinity, while mentioning none of those who preached his divinity. And so, acting consistently with what he had previously propounded, he carries over to those who are Christians regarding the humanity of Christ the following: (For he calls those who are under the Gospel here those who are among us), "Come, let us come to the word that is current among us and you, that we should serve no one but God, and that we should not attribute anything to him." Since, as we said just now, he had spoken humble things about Christ, and even called him the "God of men"—a term he also applied to himself—he now exhorts us by this common invocation of the one God, both Christians and "Agarenes" (that is, Saracens), to confess no longer that Christ is of the Father’s majesty—that is, neither consubstantial nor sitting upon the same throne. If this is granted, nothing prevents Christians from becoming Agarenes. To this extent, he brings to end the annunciation of Mary by the angels and the words and miracles of the Father to Christ.
43. But who among us would not be struck with dismay by the madness of this vain man, who calls the sister of Moses the Mother of Christ, and says that she was with Moses in the desert? Furthermore, he says that in Solomon’s temple, in the time of Zechariah, the Virgin was foretold by the angels of the birth of Christ; so that one must think that his narratives have no coherent order. For he should have considered that Solomon is prior to Moses, while Moses, Miriam, and Zechariah are later in time; and after many years from the building of the temple, that Moses wandered in the desert. O demonic intoxication! O drunk with atheism! O wretched men who believe that a book contains such falsehoods as divine! Let those who are aliens to our faith interrogate men—the Samaritans, I mean, or the Jews—let them interrogate the Greek authors and historians, if they are devoid of all logic and have blinded eyes; and let them learn from them that Moses is older than Solomon, but Solomon is more recent than Christ; indeed, Christ was not a contemporary of Moses. If Moses was not a contemporary of Christ, nor was Mary the sister of Moses, then if he deserves faith, he who proclaimed such a lie against the truth of the One who is truly God? D Truly, wickedness is blind, whether it wishes to see or to hear, and truly the one who slanders the one who is truly God is blind.
44. In his usual manner, the deceitful and mendacious one again says that the first holy house was built for men by Mahomet—
and that there are manifest signs in it in the mouth of Abraham; but nothing of this sort has been found to this day, as even those from among their number who are baptized confess. The ungodly man deceived the barbarians with his foolish sort of empty verbiage; truly, the partridge cried out and gathered eggs that it did not lay. Then, after long rantings about himself, as though speaking in the person of the savage demon breathing within him, he says the following: “Mahomet is nothing but an apostle.” For it seems that, because of his myriad false claims, he feared that someone might assume a greater opinion of him and suspect him to be either an archangel or an angel. “Mahomet,” he says, “is nothing but an apostle; and apostles passed away even before him. If he should die, the slaughter turns backward.” C How admirable is the god of the barbarian, who did not perfectly foresee what was to happen to his own apostle! But he says, “If he should die, the slaughter turns backward.” Then he states precisely: “The barbarians, who had kept the command of their God, turned backward.” Yet it was fitting that, from these things, they should discern the error brought upon them by the deceiver; and by such reflection, they ought to have assumed one or the other of these things: either that it was by some chance that we gained control of such a territory (for it is no wonder that barbarians would suspect this), or that those who previously held these places were worthy of myriad tortures because of their sins, and God, demanding from them an account of their debts through us, caused us to prevail. Yet this has nothing to do with Mahomet, even if he thrust himself into the center and appeared to be something, when he was nothing. But truly, the foolish man will not know, and the senseless man will not understand. Afterwards, he begins to instruct the barbarians for war, and he urges them always to be ready for it. “For if you fight in this way,” he says, “fear will seize your adversaries.” Having delivered these and other such warnings, and having goaded the barbarians to the slaughter of men, he brings the end to his nugatory tale.
CONFUTATION III.
45. The third chapter of the mythology of Mahomet is inscribed “The Women.” He prescribes with whom one must and with whom one must not have intercourse on account of kinship, departing little from the ancient law. Afterward, he returns to the previously laid foundation, striving that the barbarians should not think these things to be human writings, but rather things of the most high nature; and he tries to probe their thoughts, saying: “But if, as is likely, you are in doubt, saying, ‘Whence shall we know if this scripture is from God?’ create no trouble for yourselves by searching the writings or comparing them with others; but reserve them for the day of judgment, and there you shall learn whether these things are true or false.” As if to say: “Have nothing of those things by which the image of God in man is...
a book from heaven.” And indeed they asked of Moses greater things than these, and said: “Show us God manifest.” And divinity took them for their iniquities. Yet it is not written that they were destroyed by sulfur, but rather that they begged that the glory of God might not appear to them. Although this falsifier distorts all the testimonies of divine Scripture toward his own fraud and destruction. For this reason, he persuaded those who were troubled in mind concerning his tortuous fable that, if they were to seek anything of this kind from him, they would certainly perish; in another way, taking care of two kinds of persuasion, as he thought; one aiming equally at this: that he should not be disbelieved, and the other: that he might render the entire mystery of the Christian hope vain. For from this, thinking to accuse the Jews as being unbelievers in God and slayers of the prophets, he imagined that he obtained patronage for his cause from the anger of God against them, saying: “Because of the iniquity of those who Judaize, we forbade them those things which had been spoken over them; and we prepared for them a severe punishment, also because they killed the prophets against what is right.”
48. The malice of his wickedness against the mystery of the Christian religion is of this kind. He pretends, forsooth, to support Christ, and to remove the injury inflicted upon him by the Jews—that of the cross and death—so that, after he himself appears benevolent toward Christ and is thus believed, the resurrection may thereafter vanish; and this being neither done nor believed, the hopes of Christians will vanish into nothing. He says therefore: “But as for what the Jews say, 'We killed Christ Jesus, son of Mary, the legate of God,' in reality they did not kill him nor did they crucify him, but the likeness of this thing was presented to them. Truly, those who doubt about him in ambiguity have no knowledge concerning him, but only the pursuit of opinion; nor did they truly kill him, but rather God raised him to Himself.” Hence we learn that the devil transforms himself into an angel of light; and it is not to be wondered at if his servants also do the same. And now it is time to remember the words of the Lord, namely that if it were possible, even the faithful would be led into error. For you see a benevolence full of treachery, and an apology full of accusation, and a doctrine that subverts the whole economy of God. For even if he does not allow that Christ rose again as the Son of God, why not at least as a legate of God, as he himself names him? He does this for two reasons: First, because he thinks that, if I am truly a legate of God, the barbarians will demand that I too rise from the dead, just as He did; and if this does not happen to me, my bones
and the things written by me are proven to be nothing. Such is the humane shape of the inhumane one, and the staging of his myth-making; yet he is refuted by the fact that no prophetic sign is found in him.
51. Now, blathering his customary lies against the Divine Scripture, he mentions the death of Abel and says that a raven was sent to bury his corpse, while Cain looked on, and that Cain was thereby struck with compunction. These are the proper utterances of people playing on a stage, or of drunkards and madmen. Yet, in order to soften the hearts of the barbarians to receive his own blasphemies, he mentions Christ and the Gospel again, not seeing how he overturns himself. For he says: "We sent, in the footsteps of these—all the prophets, namely—Jesus the son of Mary, to confirm the truth of the law that was before him; and we handed down to him the Gospel, in which there is guidance, light, and justice, in accordance with the power of the law before him, for the guidance and admonition of those who fear, and so that the followers of the Gospel might exercise judgment. Therefore C God sent it down to him. And whoever does not judge by those things which God has handed down, these are law-breakers." Truly, do you suppose that this fool himself knows what he is saying? For if Christ came to confirm the truth of the law that preceded him, and if He provided the Gospel as a light and a guidance—as you yourself confess—and if He is the one who will perform the judgment, what remains for you to contribute to mankind? Nothing else, except that by your own testimony you demonstrate that the things which existed before you were false, and you offer darkness and a misleading guide to mankind, and at the time of judgment you will be condemned along with those who have accepted you.
52. A little later, relapsing as it were into his own madness, he says this: "O you who believe (he cries out to the barbarians), do not be supporters of the Jews or the Christians. For whoever of you patronizes them, he is one of them." These are the honors you bestow upon the people living under the evangelic guidance of Christ, for the sake of piety toward God, as you were saying. Fitting indeed; for what else would the devil and his satellites offer to their enemies? Let it be that he wishes the Christians, who confess the all-creating and life-giving holy Trinity, to suffer this insult from him; but as for the Jews, who claim to hold the faith of Abraham whole and intact, why does he inflict equal injury upon them? Yet even this is not foreign to his malice. For he knows that the law demonstrates the divinity of Christ; and for this reason he commands them to be enemies to both, because he is generally a fighter against God. That he writes these things—and the former ones, or rather all of them—not without delirium, will be clear from this. For he appends other similar things to these, and addresses the Jews and Christians thus: "O people of the Book, you are nothing as long as you do not follow the law and the Gospel, and that which has been handed down to you by your Lord." For there is added, from those things which were handed down to us by the Lord, error and infidelity. What are we now to say regarding
of the Lord as a delusion and a denial. What then, brothers, shall we say regarding these things? In truth, our discourse is with madmen, who have no sense of either good or evil. In the same way, regarding these, he lied and spoke foolishly about many other things, and he dared to slander Christ from the humility of His incarnation, and he accused monks and priests because they are filled with deep compunction and tears upon reading his writing, which is irrational; and taking up again concerning Christ, he brought Him in being reminded by God of the signs which he says He performed through Him. And he is asked if he allowed men to hold himself and his mother as gods, and he denies it, as if by this denial he said he had proved that he is not God, and [he seeks] to cause Christians to stop confessing Christ as God. But where did this most ignorant man read these things? Or through what divine epiphany did he learn them? Or, rather, it is more appropriate to say that he neither read nor learned any of the things he ought; but according to the display of his own ravings, having smeared himself with Manichaean filth and darkness, he has exhaled the stench of these things, and in these he has ended his fourth essay.
CONFUTATION V
53. The fifth myth of Mohammed is entitled "Concerning Birds"; for he seems to discuss again clean and unclean animals, and he urges the barbarians to eat all animals, except the pig; and [he permits] them to partake without fear of all beasts of burden, both donkeys and mules and camels, and absolutely any four-footed creature. "And do not follow," he says, "the way of Satan," meaning by Satan, clearly, he who delivered the ancient legislation, or rather, even the evangelical proclamation. For the rest, his whole purpose in this essay is to struggle against there being any unbelief that these prestidigitations have been received from God. But those little sayings of his which seem to establish these things not only demonstrate nothing, but they even cause aversion because they proceed from ignorance—he is of an unilluminated mind and one deprived of divine radiance, and the matter itself has no nature to sustain [itself]. For it is a repetition of words, the same things again according to the same, and from the same toward the same; and mentioning Christ, he mentions Him in no less that way which we have described. And thus, babbling, he concludes his foolish tale.
CONFUTATION VI
54. The sixth myth of Mohammed is inscribed "Concerning the Signs," that is, concerning the demonstrative tokens and proofs that such a writing was delivered to him by God. And straightway from the beginnings, the demon that spoke to him chants this to him, so that he too may seem, as is likely, to be ordered to say these things, and thus become unsuspected of deceit. For he teaches him the primeval origin of men, that they were created from clay according to the [divine] image.
and their substance originate from mud according to the image, and that all the angels were commanded to worship Adam; and while the others worshipped, Beliar alone refused to pay homage; and that when asked why he did not worship, he gave as the cause that Adam was created from mud, but he himself from fire; and that he was rebuked by God, and that he contradicted God, and threatened Adam because of the indignation he felt toward him, saying he would leave none of his descendants alive except a few who would be saved. He continues by narrating the history of Paradise and the exile of Adam. Well then, you who have been nurtured in the Holy Scriptures, you certainly understand the wanderings and foolish additions of this deceiver.
55. His aim in this matter is to say that those who disregard the commandment of God perish, while those who keep it are saved; and he maintains this sequence for his own followers, constructing it starting from Adam and those who followed thereafter, hoping that for this reason his own words will be trusted. Immediately, therefore, he introduces some unknown and absurd ravings from hidden and anonymous persons who came to preach before Noah; and wherever they were believed, they are said to have brought about divine blessing, but where they were disbelieved, a very great curse. After Noah, he introduces a certain prophet and messenger named Zalet, the brother of Thamoth, a man who never existed and was never named by anyone; yet he came to this same office and, having preached, he too made God propitious to those who believed him, while those who were unbelieving perished, he says, being cursed in their own dwellings by an earthquake. After him, he speaks of another prophet and messenger, a Midianite sent to his own nation, Saic by name; that he likewise testified and acted according to those before him—men whom neither Moses nor any of the illustrious authors recognizes. After these, he mentions Moses and says that in matters where he was obeyed, the aid of God preceded him, but where he was disobeyed, judgment consumed those responsible. And having used such a line of reasoning, he speaks immediately of himself: 56. "O people, I am a messenger of God to you all." Well done indeed! Carrying with you no work of divine power, nor any wisdom or virtue worthy of mention, you have taken upon yourself, by self-appointment and without any proof or earlier announcement, the status of a messenger. But who, even among the most foolish and those utterly devoid of the knowledge of God, is unable to do this? If, because Moses is a legislator, you must be one too, why should Pharaoh not also be a legislator? But as for the things you thought you were doing, even if it was for evil, you should at least have mentioned them as a plausible argument; but since you had nothing to boast of, you passed it over in silence. I, however, will remind you of this: Moses was not only a legislator, he was also a prophet, and we see the outcomes of his prophecies daily. What of yours shall we admire? Your debaucheries? Your slaughters? Your arsons? Your plunderings? Your blasphemies? And A what of the stoning of the holy Scriptures? For your words are more wretched than stones. God, he says, [am I] an apostle; for he is truly an apostle of God, who is a god-hater, a slanderer of the divine guide—who possesses nothing more than that which the apostolic word teaches; even if he attempts to put to shame those who hear him, through the name of the truly divine, [he is] a reprobate, and he blasphemes and subverts God himself, and attributes to Him things that even the nature of things does not allow. For how shall Satan exist, who destroys Satan along with you? or are not the commandments of God called His ways, which you say are the ways of Satan?
57. After this, as a messenger and prophet of the father of lies, he mingles in the miracle of the splitting of the rock certain spurious myths of his own, so that he might appear to be Moses to the simple-minded, and an accurate historian; and therefore he adds: "And out of it gushed twelve fountains." And shortly after he adds: "And when the stubborn Jews were in it, we said to them: Be as apes, and some were made apes, bound in bridles." Thus, although he was using a way of speaking that was not altogether unseasonable, he adds the same things as commandments. Again, however, he returns to those among whom his entire speech is woven with blasphemies, namely that God is the cause of evils, saying: "Whom God leads, he is the directed one; but those whom He makes to err, they are the ones who are deficient." And soon after, he confirms this, saying: "The soul has no power to benefit or harm itself, except what God wills." But he forgets that he formerly called himself a prophet, and says: "If I knew everything, I would certainly be multiplied in good, and no evil would touch me. I am nothing but a herald and evangelist to the people who believe." Then he rebukes them for some idolatrous deeds; for he always assumes this persona for himself, and with the addition of some detestable and absurd things, he breaks off his nonsense.
CONFUTATION VII.
58. The seventh fable of Mohammed is entirely redundant with human slaughter and killing; for it is titled The Division of Spoils. And indeed he incites the barbarians in every way to act like barbarians against the Christians; especially as he prepares them, saying that to those who perish fighting near them, a reward is to be given by God in paradise, and to his messenger; for it was not right that the spoils should have share in the cause of perdition, but rather it was fitting that the bloodthirsty people and the more bloodthirsty messenger should have blood-stained deeds. And he himself ironically bids them use irony. To those of us who ask about this and similar things, he was silent. It must be known, however, that he is aware that it happens to us because of the
REFUTATORY 8
59. The eighth myth (95) is inscribed to Muhammad, 'On Repentance'; it exhorts the barbarians to wait for a certain appointed time as to when the Christians might wish to settle their own affairs. If they should become masters of themselves and separate from the domination of the barbarians, he says to wage war against them; but if they repent, he says, and worship his impiety, to receive them as brothers. He therefore exhorts the Christians that it is better to repent and live than to die as Christians. And again, he shamelessly strikes the wretched barbarians with threats, reproofs, and insults for being sluggish in his worship; and in addition to these, according to the previously mentioned craftiness, he attempts to silence both in one refutation, Jews and Christians, calling them thus:
60. « The Jews say (96) that Israel (97) is the son of God; and the Christians say that Christ is the Son of God; this is their word through their mouths; they sound the same as the words of those who denied [Him] from before; God will slay them, for the sake of what they deny; they forgot their apostles and the priests as lords besides God; and Christ the son of Mary, and they were not commanded to serve except one God. » The barbarian is ill-minded, since he is ignorant and uneducated; for if any rational understanding were present in him at all, he ought to have proclaimed the things pertaining to this name of the Son, and to have rebuked those who are not very strongly opposed to him more mildly; but on the contrary, [he rebukes] those who contend with him the most; for the Jews do not call Israel the genuine Son of God, but only as much as will demonstrate the affection of God toward them, which is superior to the other nations because of the fathers; how then [can he be] the son of the father of...
61. And from the infinite [number] of the earlier God-seeing and prophetic Scripture; for you will find proofs rivaling the number of the stars, from the very first book of Moses to Daniel the prophet and the rest. For that Moses knew the oneness of the divine nature, and the distinction of the properties, that is, of the hypostases, is manifest; when he wrote, «Let us make man according to our image and likeness.» And when he said, «Behold, Adam has become as one of us.» But also when he wrote, «Hear, O Israel, the Lord thy God, the Lord is one,» he knew the Trinity to be understood by the cause and the essence and the concord; for it would be contrary to reason if he wrote, having set forth above that different persons were conversing, but afterwards proclaimed that there was only one person who was speaking and hearing and acting; which is unlawful. And of all the prophets of old, Daniel, who is the most manifest, clearly indicated both the time of the coming of Christ, and signified His dignity, when he says: «I was watching, and behold, on the clouds of heaven as a son of man; and to him was given the honor, and the worship, and the rest»; even though the Ancient of Days was sitting on the throne.
62. But let the things from the ancient Scriptures be dismissed. [Consider] the predictions of Christ Himself, and the fulfillment of all this, if the vain man were able to understand, [or] that which is from the testimony of John, whom he himself proclaims and accepts as a prophet and a just man; or even from the miracles which are His symbols, I mean, the tomb and the resurrection; but the leader of the host has perceived none of these; for there was never any divine care or knowledge in him, except for that of...
64. Since he himself, somewhere else, while overturning the opinions of the Greeks and the Hebrews, converses with those of his own error, which we call truth, but know to be the error of an unclean spirit; it is therefore necessary for us also to bring to light his words, as being distorted from those of others. For this deceiver says in the seventh writing of his own Alcoran: «Say, O Israelites, God has not begotten, nor has He been begotten; nor has anyone become equal to Him.» O the folly! O the unmixed and impious deception! For if He did not beget, He is not a Father; and if He is not a Father, He does not have a Son. And if he openly confesses that God is not a Father, how does he not reject Jesus as the Son? But yet, in other writings, he calls Christ the Son of God. Therefore, he declares things contrary to what he himself wrote, as if from God. And if the things written are contradictory, are they not false? For everything that fights against itself falls away from the truth. And if the things of the Scripture are true, why is he not led to the confession of the truth? But also regarding the faith of Abraham, which he puts forward as the rule of his own error, he adds: «Abraham was not a Jew, nor a Christian, but he was of a straight and upright faith, and he was not of the polytheists.» It is clear, therefore, that he does not even know what he is saying, or whom he is thus addressing; for whether Abraham was a Jew, the God of the Jews is justly God; or whether he was a Christian, we justly worship Him. But since he was neither of these, but only a friend of God, as he himself says; yet we also worship the same God and Father of Christ, and the God of Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, and nothing else. What then is the paradox, whether Jacob or Abraham, [if] we worship the same God? How then are we not heirs of His grace, we who hold his faith? But the vain-minded one says: «Say, God is not the Son of God.» O the blindness! For he himself is not ashamed to confess in other places: «In heaven is the Gospel, that Christ is God, and the Son of God.» How then is He not [the Son] here, and yet is truly [the Son] there, and here is truly [God], and there is falsely [God]? Either that inscription is false, and he spoke this truth, or this is false, and that is true. For in the same things, both the true and the false cannot coexist; but it is necessary for one of them to be true, and the other to be false.
REFUTATORY 9
69. The ninth myth of Muhammad is inscribed to Jonah, but nothing of Jonah is contained in it; rather, straight from the prologue, as if fighting and accusing or mocking, he enters saying: 'Is it strange to men that God revealed to a man from among them to bear witness to men and to bring good tidings to those who believe that there is for them a true advancement from their Lord?' He says these things as if from the person of God concerning himself; then he introduces those who, as is likely, disbelieve and say, 'This is a sorcerer'; this he says on his own behalf. We, however, regarding the first statement, do not say it is at all strange that it should be revealed by God and good tidings brought to those who believe that there is for them a true advancement from Him; provided that the phrase 'true advancement' holds true concerning Him. But since this lie has been exposed, we turn away from the one who says that something of this sort has been revealed, as truly lawless and, as he himself says, a sorcerer; for we knew Abraham to be such, and we are not surprised; for God wishes all men to be saved and to come to the knowledge of the truth; for from the ignorance of idols, Abraham advanced to a true advancement of the knowledge of God, through this being revealed to him by God; and the works of piety became clearer to him, confirming the knowledge of the truth. After him, we again knew Moses to have been revealed by God to bear witness to men that there is for them a true advancement for those who believe from their Lord; and this came to pass; for from the knowledge of God and way of life that was still imperfect regarding Abraham, the law of God brought his descendants to a more perfect knowledge of God and way of life; for if this is not granted, all things of the law are superfluous and untimely, both the Sabbath and the God-accepted sacrifices, which Abraham did not know, which is impossible. After Moses, to use the name of revelation, until the outcome should reveal the whole, God revealed through Jesus Christ to men that there is for them a true advancement for those who believe from the Lord; and the word had the work as its end; for both the knowledge of God-
REFUTATORY 10.
66. The tenth myth of Muhammad is inscribed 'The Discourse of Hud' (6), and the prologue is filled with elegant boasting, because, he says, it was made wise and versified and distinguished by a wise man; yet for the time being, he admits that his own Scripture is in need of human knowledge. He begins again to strike terror into the barbarians, that these are indeed the legislations of God; such are his games; and he terrifies them, if they should not be persuaded, from
REFUTATORY 11.
67. The eleventh myth of Muhammad is inscribed 'To Joseph,' and it narrates the things concerning the chaste Joseph, in some places deficient, in others interpolated and strained and ridiculous and shameful (8): 'And that,' he says, 'Rachel fell at his feet along with Israel his father in Egypt.' Yet Moses says she died earlier at the birth of Benjamin; thus he strives to show the seer of God always lying; and he adds, 'This we reveal to you from the narrations of those who were absent through Arabic letters.'
REFUTATORY 12.
68. The twelfth little myth of Muhammad is inscribed 'To the Thunder'; it reminds the hearers that God made heaven and earth, and He himself sends rain, and brings down hail; as if it had never been rumored before that God made these things, and commanded them to act; and then, having added these as certain agreed-upon principles, he appends his own necromancy as if it were necessarily bound to follow, and raves about such things: 'God thunders, from this know that I am His prophet; God lightens, know that I am His prophet; God placed in the earth every animal and plant; know that I am His prophet.' See from where he spoke to the wretched barbarians. But we, having learned from this, and observing the details, can speak about him according to what he himself cleverly teaches: 'A donkey brays; know, O barbarians, that Muhammad is the prophet of his God; oxen low, know, O barbarians, that Muhammad is the apostle of his God'; and likewise concerning the barking of dogs, and the buzzing of insects, and the like; to which of the impious should I liken him? Because of the excess of his fighting against God, I do not know; and the one who is deranged does not even perceive his own godless babbling, but even prides himself greatly upon it, that he supposedly hears God thundering and lightening and sees, and tells these things to others; let him publish this great and extraordinary work as if from the mouth of the one who blinded his intellectual senses, thus:
69. 'We sent you to a nation before whom nations have passed away, to recite to them what has been revealed to you.' O, the supreme wisdom of Muhammad! Truly now it is timely to say that Scriptural saying: 'The fool will speak foolish things, and his heart will conceive vain things'; but concerning the thunder, for which the inscription is also set, he says: 'And the thunder glorifies in His thanksgiving'; that is to say, the thunder is a well-disposed and rational voice; this is not of the approved Scripture; and reasonably so; for it would be necessary to think this also concerning the roar of the sea, and the whistling of the wind, and many other things; but there will be some very discordant noises, to which the aforementioned will not be appropriate; and having mentioned more shamelessly his blasphemous opinion which posits God as the cause of evils, just as indeed of good things, he says: 'For if God had willed, He could have guided all men; and whom God leads astray, there is no guidance for him.' Truly this
REFUTATORY 13.
70. The thirteenth (13) myth of Muhammad. It is inscribed to Abraham, but it explains nothing worth mentioning about the man; rather, following the myth before this, it says a few things about creation. Having listed that it is governed by the divine Spirit, he adds the things managed by Him; and he speaks the usual blasphemy, that God leads astray whom He wills, and guides whom He wills; and again he mentions Thamud and 'Ad, according to his usual nonsense; and that "For this reason I also ought to be believed as an apostle of God." And he says this is the mark of his apostleship: "The faithful, he says, will be in the judgment (14) laughing; but the infidels, he says, in the judgment—their shirts will be of pitch, and fire will cover their faces." And thus, mocking the fearful day, he departed from his prattle. It must be noted that it is characteristic of evildoers not to set out their snares openly; for otherwise they would have been caught themselves. One should not be surprised if even among his writings something praiseworthy is seen; but one must examine the things that are disgraced by him, unless he strongly fights against the very things from which his baits and deceits were taken, which are the God-written laws.
REFUTATION XIV.
71. The fourteenth fable is entitled *Noger*, which he mentions toward the end of the fable, namely, that he was a companion of the Sodomites. The entire scope of this fable is that belief in him should not be denied; therefore he says: “They will surely say, ‘You are a demoniac,’ if you affirm that you have received these things from God. For if you were truthful, you would have brought angels.” He himself sets forth two reasons why an angel should not arrive: first, because it is prohibited by certain people; and second, because upon their arrival, all things would be consumed. What grace, then, would there be for men? “But if we were to open the very heavens,” he says, “they would surely gaze upon them in amazement.” Therefore, none of these things happens. But in their place, he says, knowing the celestial things well, “Behold the height of heaven and the stars, and the shooting of the stars, at the time when Satan ascends, desirous to hear what is spoken in heaven. Therefore believe that I am an emissary; for no one before me taught this.” Then he mentions again the creation of Adam, and the origin of Belial, and his disobedience, and also Abraham, and the history of Lot. Then, having inserted some things taken from elsewhere, and considering that by this narration he had reconciled belief to himself, he subsequently rebukes those who attribute a companion to God. And thus he puts an end to the empty fable.
REFUTATION XV.
72. The fifteenth fable is entitled *The Bees*, and indeed he mentions bees around the middle of the fable, because they make wax and honey; yet he adds his own invention, namely, that these things were delivered to him by God. And he seems to touch upon the power of God in creating, and that God made the beasts and rules all things; and he swears a great oath by the name of God, that as many as say that God has daughters—the angels—will pay the penalty; the full explanation of which prediction you shall see, not without amazement, in his succeeding fable. Again he mentions the eating of meats, saying: “Do not say that this is permitted, but that is forbidden, lest you utter a blasphemous lie against God”; labeling the law of God a blasphemy and a lie; and he exhorts them to imitate Abraham in the belief of the one God. For he says, “He established the Sabbath over those who were in disagreement about it.” Yet Abraham was ignorant of the observance of the Sabbath. Whence you may infer from this that he also wanted the barbarians to observe the Sabbath; but since they perhaps would not listen to his urging, he did not dare to establish anything regarding it. And with these things his fable concludes.
REFUTATION XVI.
73. The sixteenth fable is inscribed...
[Here the text breaks off, to be continued on the following page.]
when Moses calls them "sons of God," it was his concern that no one should concede such a thing to him; and just as if he were possessed, he exchanges the characters/subjects and the forms, and instead of the "daughters of men" he fabricates female angels, according to the Greek myth:
*Iris then came as a messenger to white-armed Helen* (22)
—and in place of the "sons of God" he takes the "sons of men." And thus he bestows the nations of his own senselessness and blasphemy upon the wretches of the barbarians.
75. That which is most pitiful of all is that he proclaims this to have come from God. "For the Lord our God chose sons for you; and He took females from the angels. But you speak great words." He refers to those who speak about God, as if to say, "You claim that God has sons, and that He has made these the sons-in-law of men; whereas in fact He has rather given female angels as brides to the sons of men." Such things he says, as if for the correction of the Mosaic history. For this reason, in the preceding myth, he swears that female angels are not the daughters of God; nevertheless, he contends stubbornly that they are female angels. But alas, me, the greatest sinner of all flesh! For I am compelled, since I wish to confute the enemy of God, to speak these and suchlike words. What, then, will the God-denying Saracens say further to these things? Will they contend after this to call Mohammed a "seer of God"? Or will they not rather spit upon him, as having been revealed as a charlatan, a magician, and a deceiver? Having babbled and blasphemed even more similar things, as though he had already made them clear and irrefutable demonstrations, he adds: "And say: Praise be to God who has not taken a son!" And with these words, he puts an end to his fable. C
CONFUTATION XVII.
76. The seventeenth little fable of Mohammed is inscribed, *To the Companions of the Cave*. It relates the flight, the death, and the resurrection of the seven holy youths of Ephesus at the cave. This unlearned and barbarian man records them for two reasons: one, that he might appear expert in many scriptures; the other, that he might claim for his own dogma the miracle that befell them. But because he knew clearly that they were Christians and had been preserved whole after death for many years, he feigned a laudable ignorance; and by framing an assumption regarding God that is safely shielded—namely, that He Himself knows what He does and how He does it—he thinks to avoid the investigation of many matters, which he could not have grasped even if he had wanted to. Nevertheless, he does not even let them be without blame. For he says: "You would think them awake, but they are asleep; and you turn them to the right and to the left, and their dog extending its arms in the dust. The Lord knows their affairs."
OF THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL
The author of this work declares that "We gave the Scripture to Moses," and that "We rendered judgment upon the children of Israel in the Scripture, that there would again be desolation in the land, and that a great haughtiness would arise." Consider from what follows whether he truly gave the Scripture to Moses, for progressing only a little further, he speaks foolishly, saying: "And we have fastened the share of every man about his neck, and there will come forth to him in the day of resurrection a writing that will meet him: Read your writing." It is evident to almost everyone that Moses not only did not teach fatalism and necessity, but did not even deign to mention them. If, therefore, it pertains to the glory of God to admit fate, it is no wonder that things which were once kept in silence were delivered to Mohammed. But if he tries to prove God is the author of evil just as he is of good, he proves that laws were given and threats of punishment were invoked in vain; but this is impossible. The writer, therefore, necessarily lies in asserting that this inept fable comes from the One who gave the law to Moses.
The following passages have the same aim. He says: "When we determine to destroy a town, we command its inhabitants to live luxuriously; and thus the sentence upon them was just: We destroyed them by extermination; and truly we have destroyed many generations after Noah." Do these words befit the God who loves mankind, or a demon who hates mankind? Does God command luxury, or the devil? Who could ask for a clearer demonstration to be convinced that the entire book of Mohammed was produced by Satan? If some should say, "How was Satan able to destroy many generations after Noah?" we answer that it was by no means through divine power and sovereignty; but he was indeed able to do so through deception and by inciting them to provoke God to anger, which is something he is still able to do today. And in this, he speaks the truth by chance, saying that he destroys men.
If they are not convinced by these proofs, they will surely believe what follows. For, continuing the narration concerning Noah, he proceeds to connect that which was also said by Moses concerning the sons of God and the daughters of men, saying thus: "Our Lord has chosen sons for you, and has taken females from the angels. But you speak great words." Observe the impiety, observe the folly of this barbarian! For because it was said by Moses that, "The sons of God, seeing the daughters of men that they were beautiful, took them as wives for themselves," and that giants were born of them, he does not know who Moses calls "God"—namely Enos—nor who his sons are. Being at a loss, after so much labor in the preceding discourse, his entire hope manifestly collapses in this present chapter. For
Mohamed presents his god as swearing an oath of this sort: "By those that scatter, and by the burdens of the burdens, and by things that occur to the memory; for your God is one Lord of the heavens and the earth and all things between them; and Lord of the easts." Then he proclaims as if from the person of many, saying thus: "We have adorned the heaven that is near with the ornament of the stars; and we have guarded it against every rebellious Satan. They do not hear the high hosts, and they are struck by arrows, driven off from every side." He says that paradise has four rivers: of pure water, and of milk the taste of which does not change, and of sweet wine, and of strained honey. Writing these things, he reaches up to the fiftieth fable. In the fiftieth fable, he makes God swear thus: "By the lickers licking, and those bearing burdens, and those running straight, and those dividing matters," swearing, that is, the truth, and that the judgment is about to arrive. In the fifty-first fable, he swears thus: "By the mountain, and the writing inscribed on thin parchment, and the house built, and the ceiling raised, and the sea," the vengeance of the Lord shall come.
79. Then also in the fifty-second fable, which is inscribed *The Star*, he swears thus to the Arabs on behalf of Mohamed: "By the setting star; your companion has not been led astray, nor has he transgressed; he did not speak from his own will; there is nothing in him but revelation. He who is strong in power, who is easily seen, showed it to him; and he sat. He is in the higher firmament; and he approached, and was suspended, and there was an interval of two bows or closer; and he revealed to his servant what he revealed; he did not lie about what he saw within; for he indeed saw it. He delivered another descent near the *sideritin* (lote-tree)." And a little later he defines it clearly, saying: "How can the angels who are in heaven profit if their intercession is of no avail?" In the fifty-third fable, wishing to speak about the phases of the moon, and not knowing that it is darkened by the shadow of the earth, he says: "The hour has approached and the moon has been split. And if they see a sign, they turn away and say: It is a trick that is being shown, and they have acted mendaciously." And so, babbling, he proceeds up to the sixtieth fable. In the sixtieth fable, covering himself as if with the darkness of ignorance or lack of perception, he gives himself headlong into the abyss of unheard-of falsehood, and thus he falls.
And when he says (45): “O children of Israel, I am the messenger of God to you, confirming what was before me in the Law, and bearing tidings of a messenger coming after me, whose name is Muḥammad,” and together he brought to them the revelation. This is sorcery! And how can it be that one who speaks blasphemies against God, and is a sinner, can refrain from speaking upon such great matters? For if it had been true that such great things had been pronounced by Christ, they ought necessarily to be expounded. Wherefore no one assigns this utterance to any person by name, neither place, nor time, nor any compelling reason, nor why at that time it was necessary to happen, because these and similar things are far removed from those by whom truly they are said. Otherwise, even if he had simply pronounced “When Jesus said,” he would not have made any saying of Christ odious to Christians, but he spoke as one who mocks foolish boys, as if they were still ignorant children.
80. In the sixty-seventh fable he swears thus: “By the pen, and by those things which are written, you are not, by the grace of God, a demoniac; and to you there is a reward without end; and he is in a magnificent creation.” In the sixty-eighth fable, which he inscribes *Acca*, he begins: “Acca; what is Acca? And from where do you know what Acca is?” And he denies the false hour of Thamud and Ad. He babbles this, deceiving the barbarians as if he knew some unknown things; and he proceeds all the way to the seventy-fourth [fable] boasting. In the seventy-fourth, which he inscribes *The Resurrection*, he swears: “I do not swear by the day of the resurrection, nor by the soul that shapes itself.” In the seventy-sixth fable, which he inscribes *The Mission*, it begins: “By the missions ordained, and by the storm of storms, and by those spreading out the spreading, and by those separating the separation, and by the one casting forth the memorial, the apologetic and the protestation; what was promised shall come to pass.” The seventy-eighth fable he begins with an oath: “By those dragging away the rope, and by those swimming the swimming, and by those casting forth the projection, and by those moderating the business.” The eighty-fourth fable this author begins: “By the towered heaven, and by the day promised, by the witness and the witnesses, abolished are the sodalities of Alachut, which had fire for burning.” The eighty-fifth fable, this author begins: “By the heaven and the altar-like [star]. And how do you know what the altar-like [star] is? The star piercing through. For over every soul that exists, a guardian presides.” And the eighty-eighth fable begins with an oath: “By those who are pulled out, by the twisting, and by the swimmers, and by the ones leading, and by the one setting in motion the shaking, and by the day when the earthquake shall shake.” It begins the eighty-ninth fable: “By the heaven, and the day of the union, and by the witness and the one witnessed, the companions of the [ala-]
hum A (55), the fire having the burning.” The eighty-fifth fable (56) begins from an oath as follows:
“By the heaven and the altaricum (57); and how will you know what the altaricum is? The star piercing, which presides over every soul that exists.” The eighty-eighth fable (58) begins from an oath as follows: “By the morning light, and the ten nights, and the even and the odd, and the night when it passes away: is there in these an oath for a man of intellect (59)?”
81. The eighty-ninth fable begins with such an oath: “I do not swear by this region, and you are a resident in this region; and by the begetter, and what he begat, for we have fashioned man in strength (61).” The ninetieth fable begins from such oaths (62): “By the sun and its rays, and by the moon when it follows them; and by the day when it shines upon them; and by the night when it covers them; and by the heaven and what built it; and by the earth and what spread it out and the soul and what fashioned it.” Now he swears by the whole earth. The ninety-first fable begins with an oath: “By the night when it is present; by the day when it becomes clearer; by Him who fashioned the male and the female; certainly your life is manifold.” The ninety-second fable begins with an oath: “By the morning time, and the night when it is dark, your Lord has not forsaken you nor abhorred you (64).” The ninety-third fable concludes thus: “And when you have leisure, give effort that you may pray to God (65).” The ninety-fourth fable begins: “By the figs and the olives; by Sinai and the secure region. For we have fashioned man in a noble stature, and soon we depressed him to the lowest (66).” How great is the oath of the author of the humans, greater than which he swear concerning? “And we depressed him, he says, to the lowest.” Rightly indeed, it was fitting for Mohammed that this should be God. The ninety-sixth fable begins thus: “We brought him down in the prescribed night. And from where do you know what the prescribed night is? It is better than a thousand months. The angels descend, and the spirit in it by the permission of their Lord for every affair; it is peace, until the morning light rises (67).” The ninety-seventh fable is entitled, *To those who denounce*; and it seems to persuade the readers that his scripture is true, since he brings forth purified books, which contain rectitudes. However, let the listeners judge concerning their uprightness. The ninety-eighth fable is inscribed, *Earthquake*, as he cries aloud against those, just as also against Mohammed who blasphemes, that they be judged. The ninety-ninth fable is inscribed, *Those who run*, and it begins with an oath as follows:
By the snorting steeds, and those that strike fire, and those that make incursions at dawn, and stir up dust, and cleave through the midst of a host (75); for man is ungrateful to his Lord (74)."
82. The hundredth fable is inscribed, *The Calamity*. And it begins thus: "The Calamity (76), and what do you know what the Calamity is? The day when men shall be like scattered moths." The hundred and second fable is inscribed, *The Striking*, and it begins thus: "By the striking (77), and what do you know what the striking is? The day when men shall be like scattered moths." The hundred and fourth fable is inscribed, *The Elephant*, and it begins thus: "By the elephant (79), for man is wretched." The hundred and seventh fable is inscribed, *The Beetle* (81). It begins thus: "We have given you the beetle, and pray to the Lord, and if one hates you, he shall be cut asunder." Let us inquire further regarding the beetle. The hundred and sixteenth fable is inscribed, *The Bulaak* (82-83), for he who admits an unjust man will be burned in his wife's own furnace. The hundred and eleventh fable is as follows: "Say: He is God, the One, the Absolute (the *holosphyros*), who has neither begotten nor is begotten, and there is no one equal to Him." Even though the word *holosphyros* does not signify "dense and solid," which is the proper quality of a body. The hundred and twelfth fable is inscribed, *The Refuge*; and it is a deceptive exclamation, for it is as follows: "Say: I take refuge in the Lord of the dawn, from the evil of what He has created, from the evil of falling stars, from the evil of breathings in knots, from the evil of the envious one who envies." The hundred and thirteenth fable is a deceptive exclamation as well. It is inscribed, *Refuge*, and it is as follows: "I take refuge in the Lord of mankind, the King of mankind, the God of mankind, from the evil of the whisperings of demons who whisper in the hearts of men, from demons and men." And with these words, the whole of Mohammed’s mythology and fraud falls silent, putting an end to the blasphemous initial premise.
83. But if one looks at all these things by which Mohammed swears, it is easy to discern who this god of his is; nay, rather, it is evident through this intermediary that there are many such gods, and without any further proof it is manifest.
Was it [miraculously] brought about? And David, the son of Jesse, is shown to be descended from Him—is this Divine Scripture? Is this a heavenly book? O, the folly of it! O, the ignorance of the wretched barbarians!
94. Since this cursed book of theirs is completely alien and foreign to the mind of Divine Scripture; and since God Himself has judged them unworthy of His covenant—we can sufficiently recognize that the author of this camel-haired book is the demon who fell from heaven. Indeed, whatever is alienated from God migrates to the opposite, demonic side. For if it is a manifest indication that Isaac belongs to God—to the true God, I say—that He established a covenant with him ("For I shall establish," He says, "my covenant with him, to be his God and to be the God of his seed after him"), inversely, the indication that they are not the peculiar people of God—even if He is their God by way of creation—is the fact that God refused to establish His covenant with the Ishmaelites. Therefore, the devil is their god, he who pretends to be God; even though, fearing that he might fail in such a goal, he craftily keeps silent about his own name, which is infamous among all men, and instead bears the title of the true God. This is indeed the accustomed artifice of this wicked one. If, therefore, these barbarians desire to have the God of Abraham as their own god, let them abjure that impious and fraudulent book and its wicked author, that foolish man, and let them assent solely to Divine Scripture and be guided by it. But if they wish to place their book above this one, and to worship the adversary of the God of Abraham, let them be covered with shame, because they boast only in name of the God of Abraham, and they are proven to be vainly bringing forward irreconcilable things and Scriptures. For he who asserts that Pharaoh and Augustus reigned at the same period differs in no way from a madman. For this is the necessary consequence if the sister of Moses gave birth to Christ: that Christ, born two thousand years before, appeared before Pilate with such old age, and that Mary lived just as many or even more years.
XXVI. That there is no benefit for the Agarenes in circumcising themselves, and therefore thinking that they have the God of Abraham as their own God, in order to show from this that the God of Abraham is truly their God. Some of the master's commands result in profit for the subjects if they are kept; others benefit the master; others benefit both. Similarly, acts of disobedience bring harm, some to the subjects, some to the masters, and some to both. Now, it is plain to all that if anyone violates some commandment of his master and appoints himself as his own master, he still retains the hope of moving his [true] master back to his former benevolence. However, if he has altogether assigned to himself another master, he provokes an intolerable wrath from his [original] master against himself, even if he happens to have diligently performed all of his mandates. Nor does that servant demonstrate so much that he is the servant of his master by executing his will, as he declares himself to be alien from him by appointing another master for himself. [KϚ']
For what proof does the servant of a physician bring forward, that the physician is now his master, when he knows and performs the arts of his master well, as his master himself wills, but describes himself as the slave of another master? For the remaining commandments of God are nothing other than a healing and benefit for men; but faith in God redounds to His glory, as we affirm that God truly is, and that the relationship with the Lord is kept sound. Just as, therefore, the neglect of the remaining commandments becomes our own destruction, the subversion of His lordship insults the Lord Himself. When, then, will He be more severely angry with us? Surely when He is denied by us, as everyone knows.
Let us then examine this reasoning in the case of Abraham and Mohammed regarding God. God said to Abraham: "You shall circumcise the foreskin of your flesh"; Mohammed does this also. God said to Abraham: "You shall not worship any other god, except your Lord God"; Mohammed cannot bear to do this. For he worships the idol Chubar, which is very ancient in the Ethribian desert, and Macach, which they say was fashioned in the likeness of Venus. Mohammed cannot therefore have as his own God the God of Abraham, even if he bears the symbol of His commandment, circumcision, since he has transferred himself to another god. For He had ordered: "You shall worship no god except your Lord God." For simple circumcision is only the removal of the flesh of the one being circumcised, but it is not a demonstration of the relationship between servitude and lordship; just as the fact that a servant who has run away from a physician makes use of the art of medicine, after having retreated to another master, is not a proof that he is the servant of the physician. Empty, therefore, is the claim of the Hagarenes that their god is the God of Abraham; for they are shown to be lying. For even if He was once their god, nonetheless now, with Chubar and Mohammed, He is not their god.
XXVII. On the same argument.
96. Everything that truly exists is known at once by its name and its reality; but if it be divided into two—into name only and reality only—it deceives. I call "name only" that which is truly devoid of its proper reality; and "reality only" that which is devoid of its own true name, but is not, for all that, nameless. From both, indeed, a true knowledge is constituted, but from one or the other, error. And this is what causes men to agree or disagree among themselves about anything. The deception of a "name only" is twofold: when it signifies nothing at all, and when, being removed from its proper reality, it is assigned to one that is alien. That which signifies several true things is called the proper name of each, and at the same time is said to be. A thing, however, is true—and is said to be true—which preserves the account of its substance without defect and without redundancy. A true name, finally, is that which the account of the thing intends to signify. For neither does a thing, insofar as it is a thing, deceive; nor does a name, insofar as it is the name of a proper reality, lead into
error.
97. It is easy to refute this barbarian people, who rashly deceive themselves with the name of the God of Abraham; for He is not truly worshipped by them, even if they bear and acknowledge some particular marks of His, namely, circumcision and the boast that they are the people of the God of Abraham. For just as it is not enough to set forth the knowledge of a man to say he is "bipedal and mortal"—even though a man is truly bipedal and mortal—but these do not immediately define man, because they do not state the principal and proper mark of human nature; so, when these people say they have the God of Abraham as their God, and circumcise their flesh, they by no means demonstrate that very One who is properly and truly God, because they omit the symbols that denote Him preeminently. For when the leader of their nation commands things contrary to the law of the God of Abraham, does he not introduce another God under His name? And observe by what reasoning we say this deception occurs. The God of Abraham said to his sons: "You shall not adore any icon of idols, nor shall you render them worship," that is to say, as God. The God of the Agarenes said, as they claim, through their messenger: "Adore Chubar, as if he were God," though he is an idol and an image of Venus. Let us see, therefore, what is common between Abraham and Muhammad, and in what they differ. Let us also see in what things God is better characterized—being known as God by men—and in what things He seems to be, but is not; and in what things He is provoked to anger all the more.
98. In those commandments by which they are merely adorned, the manifestation of the Divine is lesser and dim; there are times when it is not even lesser, when He Himself is fought against. But in those matters wherein His glory is established, it is true and exceedingly greater; just as it is the other way around. Abraham received circumcision after he had already acknowledged God and rejected the idols of his father. When, therefore, did he please God more? By the negation of idols, or by the circumcision of the flesh? It is manifest that it was by the negation of the cult of idols; for it was not as anything else that he received the sign of the circumcision of the foreskin except as a sign of faith. The trickster messenger of the Agarenes also introduces circumcision, but he also introduces idolatry. Did he, therefore, also please God like Abraham? Or did he rather provoke His anger? Or, as it seems, did he perform both? But if he performed both, did he please God by circumcising, or did he provoke Him much more by worshipping idols? It is manifest that he provoked Him all the more; for those whose observance is of greater importance, for those same, an equal violation is worse. For the nature of things is known either from the things themselves or from their circumstances. And the knowledge from circumstances is weaker, but that which emerges from the things themselves is safer and more sincere. One demonstrates the nature of servitude and lordship by the thing itself, when the relation is kept immutable; but not by the thing itself, when one shows something pertaining to the matter, but not the ...
relation; it possesses it not. Thus, circumcision is about the relation of servitude, but is not the relation itself. But idolatry not only has no divine relation in itself, but it opposes it and separates itself therefrom. Hence it is clear who is near to God, Abraham or Mohammed: which law exhibits the God of Abraham; is it that which says, "Thou shalt not adore another god, but the Lord thy God"? or is it this one [of Mohammed] which bids one to adore Chubar, the image of Venus? Let them not, I pray, glory in the name of the God of Abraham, while they believe things contrary to the God of Abraham, and are thus deceived, as if they had the God of Abraham as their own proper and benevolent God. For they boast of the name alone, without the reality, and falsely worship it by assigning it to a different and unalike being. Certainly, they err by taking the indicative mark from the circumstances; but by the thing itself they are convicted of being strangers to true worship. Unless perhaps we seem to be forced to have one God, while they confess two entirely contrary gods, a true and a false one; or, what is more consonant with the truth, they possess neither.
XXVIII. Demonstration that Mohammed does not introduce the truly real God, nor do the Agarenes worship Him, but an apostate demon who has usurped the divine appellation for himself.
XXIX. That the Divine is surpassingly removed from all beings, if indeed anything else characterizes it, is clearly defined by its being immutable and unchangeable, both in regard to its being, and to its thinking, and to its speech, and to its acting; as it is written: "All shall be changed, but Thou art the same." Likewise: "The counsel of the Lord abides forever." Likewise: "Forever, O Lord, Thy word abides." Likewise: "All Thy works are true." It is clear that these are to be understood in a more divine rather than a more human sense. It must be considered now whether the camel-driver, the legate of the Devil, who cobbled together a third and neoteric testament, concedes that these things are maintained in the Divine.
C. Since the Divine is admitted to be entirely immaterial and incorporeal, yet contemplated through analogy in His works, and recognized through the divine words, when the words conform to the works, and celebrated also by His magnificent volitions, when these are manifested by Him—which likewise follow their own purpose through word and work—if anything contrary to the others is found among these, he who clings to this error will be seen to have fallen completely from the true religion of God. Let us observe this now in the Arab, regarding the Pharaoh and the Red Sea; I know not why—unless, as I think, that saying, "Sinners shall fall into their own net," he himself has experienced. But he rejects the law of Him who divided the sea; certainly, by rejecting the volitions after the words, he openly constitutes another God for himself by his own choice, but certainly not the true God. For He who divided that sea, for no other reason
but that his sermons should be observed. Therefore, he who abolishes his sermons abolishes the author himself; he who abolishes the author denies at the same time the miracle performed by him. Yet since Muhammad confesses that miracle, he must surely say it was performed by another god, not by Him who is the author of the sermons in which this very miracle is narrated. Wherefore Muhammad's impiety is double, or rather triple; for he strips the miracle from God the author, and because he attributes it to one by whom it was not performed. Who, moreover, is the one who from the beginning has exalted himself against the divine glory, we know well. It is necessary, therefore, that this one be worshipped by Muhammad, since he thinks in such a way. Or, according to Muhammad’s opinion, God has surely been changed and has turned; and His word no longer remains for eternity, nor are all His works true. But may this absurd blasphemy be cast back upon the heads of the guilty. But if God remains unconvertible and immutable, rather let Muhammad be perverted, who impiously blasphemes God as changed and turned, and destroys His sermons. We, however, partake of the grace of divine steadfastness, we who accept His miracle, and observe and carry out His laws, and from them we learn and understand lessons worthy of God and prophecies both concerning Christ and all things present and future.
101. From these things, therefore, it is concluded that Muhammad must be convicted of saying either that God does not exist, or at any rate that there is a different god; or if He does exist, that He does not remain in His own state, but, as happens to those things that come into being and subsequently suffer corruption, He departs from His own habit of life, changed by time: how great the impiety of this opinion is, is easily discerned. Whoever loves the truth above all should use this rule: even if Muhammad admits some other thing from either the Old or the New Testament—for he himself dares, even if unwillingly, to confess the virgin birth—let him convict him from his own confession. For the barbarian is very unlearned and uneducated.
XXIX. That those are mistaken who say that the nation of the Hagarenes retains the worship of God the Father.
102. Since our Lord Jesus Christ is called and is the Truth, it is necessary for those who have believed in Him not to concede that the God of Abraham, and the God of Moses, and the God of Aaron is worshipped by the Hagarene nation. For the Lord says: He who does not honor the Son, does not honor the Father; clearly as all those holy patriarchs and prophets remembered and worshipped the Son of God, foreknown. For Christ says: Abraham, your father, rejoiced that he might see my day, and he saw it and was glad. Moses wrote about him; and the subsequent prophets, partly by work and partly by words, mystically revealed him. How then does this impious nation, which toward [destructive]
destruction of the Son aspires, worship His Father? Or how would the Father Himself accept that the One who is truly His Son and Word and God should, by their blasphemous mouths, be seen to be consigned to non-existence? No father is a friend to the enemies of his only-begotten; for if they truly worshipped the Father, the Father Himself would have revealed the Son to them. For the Lord says: "No one knows the Son except the Father, and anyone to whom He chooses to reveal Him"; which we see having happened in the case of the ancient saints, who left behind their knowledge and faith concerning the Son written in scrolls as if on pillars. Otherwise, how could they have come to know the Father, if the Son did not reveal Him to them? "No one knows the Father except the Son"; so that all the distinguished and righteous men of the Old Testament, worshipping God and Father, remembered and were initiated into the knowledge of the Son of God.
B ργ'. And the Son has given this clear sign, both for them and for those after them who worship the Father, that one must honor the Son; for we see this truly established both in the Law and in grace. It is evident, therefore, that the Agarenes do not worship the God of Abraham and Moses, the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, since they deny the knowledge of the Only-Begotten; but rather, they worship the father of lies—as this follows by necessity, because they do not worship the Father of Truth.
